§67 — A failed catalogue read is not an absent catalogue

Why. On 2026-10-19 two composed reviews drew a blank model cell on the pane: r-20261019T124902820946-review-of-a-derivation-builds-the-ledger-index-once-… and r-20261019T125023542204-review-of-the-project-c-clause-can-fail-…. A third review, r-20261019T125341832344-review-of-a-launch-names-its-composers-interpreter-…, drew a model. All three fell back from the picker onto clive. The two blank ones were launched with no --model, so the CLI's own default ran, and their agent records read model: None. The third passed --model deepseek-v4.1-flash. The difference is the image the dispatching follower was running. The blank ones carry RECKON_FOLLOWER_IMAGE_COMMIT=f6bd75b0…, a reload image materialised at 12:48:07Z that holds reckon/ and skills/ but no docs/. The named one carries f71e8248…, materialised at 12:52:10Z with the model catalogue. f6bd75b0 is exactly the tree git ls-tree <commit> reckon skills | git mktree yields for every commit on main from 12:36Z to 12:55Z. It is the identity without the catalogue folded in.

The cause is in reckon/checkout_trust.py. _committed_catalogue_blob returns None both when HEAD carries no catalogue and when its git rev-parse fails: any OSError or SubprocessError, a timeout included, and any non-zero exit. _committed_reload_tree reads that None as a checkout without a catalogue and returns the package-and-scripts tree. So one failed read under load, which is common on this storage this afternoon, changes the code identity. Every follower and producer then reloads into an image without the catalogue, and every launch it composes until the next good read names no model. When the read next succeeds, the identity changes back and they reload a second time. The blank model cells this plan's §63 fixed for stale images return for that window, and each such read costs the fleet two spurious reloads. committed_reckon_tree folds a failed read the same way.

Deliverable. A failed read is distinct from an absent catalogue. The catalogue reader reports absent only when git answers that HEAD has no such path, and reports a timeout, an OSError or any other failure as unknown. When any read in the identity computation is unknown, nothing reloads and nothing materialises an image from the partial read. The reload decision is _FollowerReloader.poll in reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, which reaches the identity through runs.follower_code_stamp and compares it with its recorded stamp by equality. An unknown reading is therefore treated there as no change, never compared as a value. Any other reloader the node finds consuming the same identity, such as the producer's, gets the same treatment. A checkout that genuinely lacks the catalogue still yields the package-and-scripts identity, as a wheel install requires. If a reloader the node must change lives outside the named files, it stops blocked and names that file.

Done when a test in a temporary repository whose HEAD carries the catalogue makes the catalogue read time out. It finds that the identity computation reports unknown rather than returning the catalogue-less tree. _FollowerReloader.poll, given that reading, does not reload and keeps its recorded stamp. A second case, a repository with no catalogue at HEAD, still yields the package-and-scripts identity. A third case, with the catalogue present and every read succeeding, yields the identity with the catalogue folded in, as today. The negative control restores the folding of a failed read into absent: the first case then returns the catalogue-less tree, and the reloader reloads. Every log is written under the run directory and ends in EXIT=. The test files importing reckon/checkout_trust.py or reckon/crew_follow_commands.py show no added failure against base, recorded as baseline_suite and after_suite objects. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the changed Python files. Write paths: reckon/checkout_trust.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py and tests/test_a_failed_catalogue_read_is_not_an_absent_catalogue.py.

§68 — A discarded request is never reported as queued

Why. On 2026-10-19 power_convertor's coordinator discarded the queued review r-20261019T213210926928-review-of-whole-pulse-data-for-figures-4649c6d6b72d, and its discard record is stamped 21:40:43Z. The project's watch stream then carried a queued → discarded row at 21:40:48Z. At 21:40:49Z it carried a second row naming the same run as queued: "event": "baseline", from_state null, session_counts null, and the hold detail the request was queued with. The follower drew "new → dropped → queued", and the coordinator reported that the review had requeued itself. It had not. No pointer, claim or launch exists for it, and the producer's own log shows it dropped with nothing after.

That row is the admit service's first-failure report. In admit_once (reckon/crew/queue_admission.py), _launch_queued releases the queued pointer before the launch while the pass holds the request in memory. When the launch does not survive, _record_queued_failure finds the pointer gone and calls _restore_queued. That function reads _discarded_since and leaves the absence the operator asked for. Because this was the request's first failure, _report_queued_failure then runs. It re-lists the live pointers, adds the in-memory request back when its pointer is missing, and appends a row with previous=None. It never asks whether the request was discarded. So a discard landing between the pass's listing and its report is drawn as a request back in the queue.

Deliverable. The first-failure report appends no row for a request whose discard is recorded. It reads that through _discarded_since, the same check _restore_queued uses, and adds no second discard test. A request whose launch fails and which nobody discarded is still reported once, as today. Change nothing else in the pass.

Write paths: reckon/crew/queue_admission.py and the node's evidence fragment. tests/test_a_discarded_request_stays_discarded.py is granted only to add cases. Reuse its _discard_mid_pass fixture, and remove or weaken none of its assertions.

Done when all of these hold:

  • The granted test file gains two cases for a request that carries no failure_reported_at, so its failure is its first:
    • The request is discarded mid-pass through _discard_mid_pass, and its launch fails. After admit_once, the project's watch stream holds no row naming the run that was appended during the pass, and no pointer is written back.
    • Nobody discards the request, and its launch fails. The stream gains exactly one row naming the run, at state queued, and the restored pointer carries failure_reported_at.
    Both cases run under the file's existing temporary crew home.
  • Run pytest in the foreground with -rA over the granted file, tests/test_a_held_request_stays_queued.py, tests/test_a_discard_keeps_the_admission_record.py, tests/test_a_queued_run_is_admitted.py, tests/test_a_queued_run_waits_on_its_lane.py, tests/test_a_jev_hold_queues_for_the_local_lane.py, tests/test_overlapping_queued_requests_launch_in_turn.py and tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py. It adds no failure against a base arm run at the dispatched base revision. Both arms' logs go in the run directory, and the manifest's baseline_suite names the base log and its failure ids exactly as pytest prints them.
  • ruff check and ruff format --check add no finding on the staged paths, judged by delta against the base blobs.

Negative control. Remove the discard check from _report_queued_failure, and the discarded case must fail on the appended row. Run it under pytest with the default short summary.

After it lands. The next request discarded while an admission pass holds it should leave no "queued" row after its discard row in the stream.

§70 — Unwatch reads the reconciled seat, not a registration mid-write

Why. On 2026-10-20 suite-green §27 (run r-20261020T203929222890-the-unattached-session-dispatch-test-passes) traced a long-standing red test to its teardown. The test called recovery.unwatch while the producer was rewriting its seat registration in place, and unwatch refused with refusing to stop watcher for project 'sample': the locked registration names ''. The test now waits for the settled record. But the cause is in unwatch in reckon/crew/recovery_watch.py: when the seat lock is held, it reads the raw registration and refuses any record whose project differs from the project asked for, an empty one included. watch_state in reckon/crew/watch_unit.py reconciles the same record against the live holder, through _fresh_watch_holder, before it reports. So a caller can see a live local producer through watch_state and still be refused by unwatch for the moment the registration is mid-write. Any coordinator or test stopping a watcher in that window gets a refusal that reads as a foreign seat.

Deliverable. unwatch decides whether a held seat is this project's from the same reconciled reading watch_state uses, not the raw registration. A seat whose registration is empty or mid-write while the live holder is this project's own producer is unwatched normally. A seat naming another project, or a foreign host, is still refused as today. The change lives in reckon/crew/recovery_watch.py. If the reconciliation needs a change in reckon/crew/watch_unit.py, the node stops blocked and names it.

Done when a test in a temporary crew home holds the seat lock with an empty registration while this project's producer is the live holder, and finds unwatch stops it. §73 — A reload deferral is printed once

Reported by the lead on 2026-10-21 from a crew-host pane 9h27m old ("what is all of this noise"), triaged by a peer session and handed to this plan. In the excerpt, one of eight lines was a real event: 07:19:33 implement read-correction-path waiting → working 4h27m.

Why. The pane printed reckon crew follow deferred its reload: the import proof lock was still held after 30 s; keeping the current image, probing again after 60 s. That is the follower's own image probe, from _FollowerReloader in reckon/crew_follow_commands.py (around line 564), contending with peers' landings for the import proof lock. The line is printed under a timeout_deferral flag, which already holds it to one line per checkout stamp: the flag is cleared by _clear_probe_retry, which runs when a probe answers and also when _tick sees the checkout stamp move (self.probe_retry_stamp is not None and self.probe_retry_stamp != current_stamp, around line 503). On a stamp move that call resets the retry backoff, deliberately, because a gap earned by the old checkout no longer describes the new one, and it clears timeout_deferral with it. The timeout branch then clears the flag a second time on the same tick (if self.probe_retry_stamp != current_stamp: … self.timeout_deferral = False, around line 550), reached because the first call has just set probe_retry_stamp to None. Either clear alone is enough to reprint the line. On a checkout that peers land into every few minutes, each landing is a new stamp, so a deferral that spans several landings prints a fresh line for each one. That is the repetition in the reported pane. A reader can do nothing with it: the follower keeps its image and retries on its own.

Deliverable. A stamp move still resets the retry backoff, but neither stamp-move site clears timeout_deferral: not the _clear_probe_retry call on the stamp-move branch (around line 508), and not the timeout branch's own stamp check (around line 553). _clear_probe_retry has two further callers that do clear it, and must keep doing so: the probe that answers with a genuine import failure (around line 572) and the probe that answers and lets the reload proceed (around line 587). Its caller on the unmoved-stamp branch (around line 498) runs only when no deferral is open. So only a probe that answers clears the flag. A deferral then prints one line for its whole contiguous span however many stamps pass during it, and a probe that answers and lets the reload proceed prints nothing further. This is the same shape as _defer_config_tick, whose flag is cleared by a successful read and not by the passage of time.

Done when tests/test_a_reload_deferral_is_printed_once.py holds the import proof lock while the checkout stamp moves three times, across probe retries, and finds exactly one deferral line. It then releases the lock and finds the reload proceed with no further deferral line, and a second, later deferral prints its own one line. The first case fails at the base revision with three lines, run and logged there. The negative control restores the reset on a stamp change, and the first case then finds three. Every test file under tests/ that imports reckon.crew_follow_commands or drives crew follow, plus the new file, shows no added failure against a base arm, both arms from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py passes at head, since the follower module stands near that test's bound. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, the module §75 moves _FollowerReloader into (the flag's two stamp-move clears are that class's methods), and the new test.

§74 — A queued run refused at launch names its refusal

Why. On 2026-10-21 this plan's §10 sat queued for four hours (run r-20261021T084345175245). Each time a slot opened, its launch re-checked the plan-wide review gate, and a later edit to §73 had left the plan with an unreviewed unit. So the launch failed with PlanReviewMissingError. The admission pass recorded it as refused only twice and as held-on-retry-backoff with the error in detail on 25 rows, beside 481 held-for-allowance passes (/home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/admission-verdicts.jsonl). Throughout, the obligations view described the run as waiting for a lane slot: position 1. A second run, suite-green §30 (r-20261021T121821049275), was refused four times between 12:25 and 12:46 and dropped out of the obligations list. The refusal is stamped on the queued pointer's failure field (_stamp_failure in reckon/crew/queue_admission.py), and a reader of that field already exists: _queued_failure_remedy in reckon/crew/recovery_classification.py, which feeds the obligations item queued-awaiting-session. It drops this refusal at one filter, if session not in text: return None, which keeps only failures whose text names the session, and a PlanReviewMissingError does not. The remedy is one command, and a coordinator who could see the refusal would run it at once.

Deliverable. _queued_failure_remedy surfaces a queued pointer's stamped launch failure whatever text it carries, not only one that names the session, with the remedy the failure states. For a PlanReviewMissingError, that is the reckon crew review command its message names. A failure that names the session keeps today's follower remedy. A lane hold (_is_lane_hold) is still left to the waiting reading, so a run waiting only for a slot still reads as waiting. The obligations item and the live row read this through the reader they already call; no second reader is added. Whether a queued launch should be refused at all for an uncovered unit in a different section is a design question for the plan-review gate's owner, and this section records it without settling it.

Done when a test in a temporary crew home writes three queued pointers: one whose failure is a PlanReviewMissingError that does not name the session, one whose failure names the session, and one carrying a lane hold. It finds the first's obligations item naming the refusal and its reckon crew review remedy, the second keeping its follower remedy, and the third still reading as waiting for a slot. The negative control restores the session filter, and the first pointer then reads as waiting. Every test file importing reckon.crew.recovery, reckon/crew/recovery_classification.py or reckon/crew/obligations.py, rebuilt from the head tree, adds no failure against a base arm, both arms from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory with RECKON_SESSION_HOST_SETTLE, RECKON_FOLLOWER_OWNER, RECKON_FOLLOWER_IMAGE_COMMIT and RECKON_FOLLOWER_SOURCE_ROOT unset, run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/recovery_classification.py and the new test.

§75 — The follower module fits a worker window

§76 — The pane repeats no line that carries no news

Why. On 2026-10-21 an imas-efit coordinator's pane (session 704ee605) printed yours: 1 working · nothing needs you three times in a row with nothing between. One line earlier it printed no live work in 704ee605-6100-4771-baae-9ea338ff1992 since 21:12 · 1 run needs the coordinator, directly under needs you: 1 unpromoted cbl-replay-hardening 2h17m reviewed. In this repository's coordinator session the same heartbeat, yours: 0 working · nothing needs you, arrived as three notifications in about twenty minutes, each starting a turn that had nothing to do. A coordinator woken for nothing learns to ignore the pane.

Both lines come from the follower. needs_you.DEFAULT_REMIND_SECONDS (600) re-emits the needs-you line on that cadence even when its set is empty, "so a quiet pane is a heartbeat rather than a silence a dead follower also shows". Under the session host that buys nothing, because the host's re-check pass in reckon/crew/session_host.py restarts a child follower that exits, so a quiet pane there is a quiet fleet. The settle line, _follower_settle_line in reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, names the pane's own session by its full id, which the reader already knows. Its tail, from needs_you.drain_tail, restates the count the needs-you line printed a moment earlier, without naming the run.

Deliverable. The follower prints a summary line only when it carries news:

  • the remind cadence re-emits a non-empty set only, because an unpromoted or blocked run is work the coordinator still owes;
  • the empty line, yours: N working · nothing needs you, prints once, when the set becomes empty after the pane last showed it non-empty, and never on the cadence;
  • the settle line reads no live work since 21:12, naming a session only when the pane also observes sessions other than its own;
  • the settle line's tail names the outstanding runs (· needs you: cbl-replay-hardening) only when they differ from the set the pane's last needs-you line showed, and is absent otherwise; a drained fleet still reads as drained.

The docstrings of reckon/crew/needs_you.py and the comment beside the remind loop say why the empty heartbeat is gone. The working count stays out of the set's signature, as today, so a launch or an exit re-emits nothing.

Done when tests driving the follower with a fake clock, extending tests/test_follow_needs_you_line.py, tests/test_follow_drain_line.py and tests/test_a_pane_settles_when_no_work_is_live.py, find:

  • an empty set held for 35 minutes emits no summary line;
  • a set going from one unpromoted run to empty emits the empty line once, and nothing in the 35 minutes after;
  • a non-empty set held unchanged for 25 minutes is emitted at 10 and at 20 minutes, as today;
  • the settle line for the pane's own session carries no session id, and names an observed session when the pane observes one;
  • the settle line's tail is absent when the last needs-you line showed the same set, names the runs when the set has changed since, and reads drained when nothing is outstanding.

The negative control restores the cadence for the empty set, and the first case then emits three lines. The test files importing reckon/crew/needs_you.py or reckon/crew_follow_commands.py add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py passes at head. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/needs_you.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, and the tests asserting the superseded text, which are the three files above plus tests/test_a_pane_does_not_settle_during_admission.py and tests/test_a_foreign_producer_overrun_is_one_line.py.

§77 — A follower armed after a settle continues the pane's words

Why. In the same pane, after the settle at 21:12 the session host armed the next follower for the next dispatch. Its first row for a run the pane had shown as delivered 2h17m earlier read 21:16:09 implement cbl-replay-hardening new → landed. The painter starts every run it has not drawn at new: in reckon/crew/ticker.py the first streamed row's from_state defaults to new. Its memory of what it drew lives in _reported. A re-arm restores that memory from the follow checkpoint, whose reported field carries the state last shown for each run (reckon/crew/follow_checkpoint.py, read in reckon/crew_follow_commands.py). So either the run was missing from the checkpoint the next follower read, or that follower did not restore it; which one is not yet known. The pane's checkpoint, history and delivered records, copied at 21:53 CEST, are in ~/.cache/reckon-monitor-noise/2026-10-21/.

Deliverable. First confirm from those records, and from a reproduction, which path drew new: a checkpoint written without the run, a settle that discards it, a restore that reads it as stale, or a run id that differs between the delivered row and the landed one. Then fix that path, so the first row a follower draws for a run the pane already showed starts from the word the pane last showed.

Done when a test drives follower A to draw a run as delivered and settle, then arms follower B for the same project and session as the host does, and finds B's row for the run's landing reading delivered → landed. The negative control arms B without the repair, and the row reads new → landed. The manifest names the path found, with the responsible line read from the committed blob. The test files importing reckon/crew/follow_checkpoint.py, reckon/crew/ticker.py or reckon/crew_follow_commands.py add no failure against a base arm, on the same terms as §76. Write paths: reckon/crew/follow_checkpoint.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py or the module §75 moves the code into, and the new test.

§78 — A delivery reaches the coordinator once, row first

Why. On 2026-10-21 three review runs in this repository each reached the coordinator twice. The order was backwards: first the needs-you line naming the run unpromoted, then the pane's working → delivered row for it. Times are UTC, with the manifest write first, then the delivered row, then the promotion:

RunManifest writtenDelivered rowPromoted
r-20261021T20034867014420:22:5020:23:2720:25:51
r-20261021T20242472990220:34:4720:36:4720:40:26
r-20261021T20375286896820:56:2220:57:3921:01:08

The needs-you line reads the run's live pointer and manifest directly (needs_you.needs_you_runs, scanned on the follower's sample cadence), while the row waits for the producer to append the run's transition to the stream. So the line names a delivery 37 seconds to 2 minutes before the pane draws the row that explains it. Under the session host each is a separate notification, and the second arrives while the coordinator is already acting on the first.

Deliverable. A delivery reaches the coordinator once, row first. The needs-you set admits a run only after the pane has drawn the row that put it in that state. For an unpromoted run that is the row whose pane word is delivered. For a blocked run it is the row whose pane word is halted, because the pane prints the states of FLEET_BLOCKED_STATES, which needs_you derives its blocked kind from, as halted. The two come from independent generators in the follower, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py or the module §75 moves them into. The row policy (path.feed) yields rows as stream lines arrive. _needs_you_events yields the line, sampling on needs_scan_seconds and returning early when it sampled recently, so today it does not run on the pass that drew the row. The coupling sits there. The set the line names is filtered against the words the row policy has drawn, path.reported, the same memory §77 restores across a settle. When the row policy draws a delivered or halted row for a run, the follower runs the needs-you scan on that same pass, past the sample throttle, and yields the line immediately after the row, so the two arrive together. A run whose row this follower will never draw is still admitted from the pointer once UNDRAWN_ADMISSION_SECONDS has passed since its manifest read complete. That is a named constant in reckon/crew/needs_you.py, set to 180 seconds, above the longest lag measured in the table above, 2 minutes. That covers a pane that attached after the transition, or a row the pane withheld. A run is therefore never hidden from the line. The lag between the manifest write and the row is recorded in the manifest, measured on this workstation, and is not a target of this section.

Done when a test driving the follower with a fake clock and a stubbed stream finds:

  • while a run's manifest reads complete but the stream carries no delivered row for it, no needs-you line names it;
  • when the row arrives, the row and then the line naming the run are written in one flush, in that order;
  • a run whose delivered row never arrives is not named 179 seconds after its manifest reads complete, and is named at 180 seconds;
  • a run turning blocked is not named while the stream carries no halted row for it, and when that row arrives, the row and then the line naming the run as blocked are written in one flush.

The negative control admits a run from its pointer alone, and the line then precedes the row in both the delivered case and the blocked case. The test files importing reckon/crew/needs_you.py or reckon/crew_follow_commands.py add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py passes at head. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/needs_you.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py or the module §75 moves the code into, tests/test_follow_needs_you_line.py and the new test.

§79 — A deferred run leaves the needs-you line until its deferral ends

Why. At 22:53 UTC on 2026-10-21 this repository's coordinator deferred run r-20261021T204353308966-the-pane-repeats-no-line-that-carries-no-news with reckon crew ack until 00:53 UTC, while its population at merge ran. The obligations hook dropped it at once. The pane went on naming it on the reminder cadence: needs you: 1 unpromoted the-pane-repeats-no-line-that-carries-no-news 2h12m reviewed, then again at 2h15m and 2h17m, each a notification that started a turn. The obligations view honours a deferral through _acknowledgements_in_force in reckon/crew/obligations.py. The needs-you derivation, needs_you.needs_you_runs, does not read it, so two readers of what needs the coordinator disagree, and the noisier one wins.

Deliverable. needs_you_runs leaves out a run whose obligations are deferred, until the deferral's instant. It reads the deferral through the same reader the obligations view uses, not a copy of its rules. When the deferral expires, the run returns to the set, which is a change, so the line names it once.

Done when a test in a temporary home finds:

  • a delivered run deferred until an hour from now is not named by the needs-you line;
  • at the deferral's instant plus one second it is named, once;
  • a run whose deferral has already expired is named.

The negative control ignores the deferral, and the first case then names the run. The test files importing reckon/crew/needs_you.py or reckon/crew/obligations.py add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/needs_you.py, and reckon/crew/obligations.py only to make its deferral reader public, and the new test.

§80 — A refused queued launch is not drawn as a drop

Why. On 2026-10-21 a queued implement run in this repository, a-limit-reset-is-one-record, had a launch refused with PlanReviewMissingError at 23:44:21 UTC. §81 — The review reflex waits for a resumed or deferred run

Why. Reported on 2026-10-22 by a power_convertor coordinator, for run r-20261021T234457021719-fdr-secant-rows (times UTC). The run delivered head 781e88ea and was resumed with crew resume --advice at about 02:08. The review reflex created review r-20261022T020659889981 at 02:08:26 for the superseded head. The run re-delivered head 99071202, was resumed again at about 04:13, and was deferred with crew ack at 04:15:20, until 07:30, so the review would wait for the next head. The reflex still created review r-20261022T041439122579 at 04:15:50 for the superseded head, while the run's phase was working. Both reviews were stopped and discarded by hand, each a worker's review spent on a head nobody would land.

The reflex that fires on the follower's cadence is resumption.sweep, called from reckon/crew_follow_commands.py. It calls dispatch_awaiting_reviews in reckon/crew/recovery_review_acceptance.py, which runs withdraw_superseded_review and then returns early while review_settle_seconds_remaining is above zero, before it reaches dispatch_review_for_run. The scoring loop in reckon/crew/recovery_watch.py runs only under crew recover --dispatch-reviews. The dispatcher in reckon/crew/recovery_review_dispatch.py has two refusals. _open_review_refusal prevents a duplicate open review. _resume_superseded_refusal withholds a review while the run still carries the head its resume superseded, reading supersedes_head, which compares the head with the one the resume recorded through resume_superseded_head. Between the settle window and that refusal the first case should have been covered, yet both reviews were created, 26 seconds and 2 minutes 50 seconds after their resumes. Why is not yet known. One candidate is that the settle window reads only the sweep's own auto_resume stamp, so a coordinator's crew resume --advice starts no settle window. Another is that the reflex ran before the resume recorded the head it superseded. A third is that the resume recorded no superseded head supersedes_head recognises. Nothing in the sweep or the dispatcher consults a deferral, which is the second case.

Deliverable. First find, from the run's live pointer and review-dispatch records and a reproduction driven through resumption.sweep, why neither the settle window nor _resume_superseded_refusal refused those two reviews. Name the responsible line from the committed blob in the manifest. Then repair that path, so that a run whose latest attempt is a resume that has not re-delivered is never reviewed at its superseded head, whatever the order of the resume's write and the reflex's sweep. The reflex also dispatches no review for a run whose recorded process is alive. It dispatches none for a run whose deferral is in force either, read through the reader the obligations view uses, obligations._acknowledgements_in_force, not a copy of its rules. A run that re-delivers after its resume is reviewed at its new head, as today.

Done when a test drives the reflex over a temporary crew home and finds:

  • the resume ordering the reproduction found dispatches no review for the superseded head;
  • a run with a live process dispatches none;
  • a deferred run dispatches none until the deferral's instant, and one after it;
  • a run that re-delivered after its resume dispatches one review, at the new head.

The negative control reverts the repair of the path the reproduction found, and the first case then dispatches a review for the superseded head. A control that removes _resume_superseded_refusal itself does not count, because that guard already exists at base. The test files importing reckon/crew/recovery_review_acceptance.py, reckon/crew/recovery_review_dispatch.py or reckon/crew/resumption.py add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py passes at head. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/recovery_review_acceptance.py, reckon/crew/recovery_review_dispatch.py, reckon/crew/resumption.py, which records a resume's superseded head, if the cause lies there, and the new test.

§82 — The pane prints no line that asks nothing of the coordinator

Why. The lead, on 2026-10-22: "what is the reason for even having a single yours: nothing needs you line, why alert someone when there is no action to take?" Under the session host every line the follower prints is a notification that starts a coordinator turn. §76 cut the empty needs-you line, yours: N working · nothing needs you, from every ten minutes to once per emptying. That once still wakes the coordinator to say there is nothing to do, and it usually arrives just after the coordinator's own promotion emptied the set, which the coordinator already knows. The settle line of a drained fleet, no live work since HH:MM with a drained tail, is the same: it names nothing to act on.

Deliverable. A line the follower prints names something the coordinator can act on, and the docstrings of reckon/crew/needs_you.py and the follower's summary code state that rule. The empty needs-you line is not printed at all. The needs-you line prints only when its set is non-empty and has changed, and on the reminder cadence while it stays non-empty. The settle line prints only when its tail names outstanding runs. A drained settle prints nothing, and the follower still exits with its settle code, so the session host reads the settle as today.

Done when tests driving the follower with a fake clock find:

  • a set going from one unpromoted run to empty prints nothing;
  • a drained settle prints nothing, while the follower exits with the settle code;
  • a settle with an outstanding run prints its line naming the run;
  • a change in a non-empty set prints as now, and a non-empty set unchanged for 25 minutes is reminded at 10 and 20 minutes, as now.

The negative control restores the empty line, and the first case then prints it. The test files importing reckon/crew/needs_you.py or reckon/crew_follow_commands.py add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py passes at head. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/needs_you.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, and the tests asserting the superseded lines: tests/test_follow_needs_you_line.py, tests/test_follow_drain_line.py, tests/test_a_pane_settles_when_no_work_is_live.py and tests/test_a_pane_does_not_settle_during_admission.py.

§83 — The Monitor stream carries only lines that ask the coordinator to act

Why. On 2026-10-22 the lead ruled that the Monitor stream carries only lines that ask the coordinator to act. Under the session host every printed line is a notification that starts a coordinator turn, so a line that names nothing to do costs a turn and teaches the coordinator to ignore the stream. The same night the stream repeated a twelve-item needs-you line every ten minutes for five hours, and drew launches, working/waiting churn, and landed and dropped rows for actions the coordinator had just taken itself.

Deliverable. The follower's Monitor stream carries a line only when it names something only the coordinator can do next:

  • a run of the session delivered and unpromoted;
  • a run halted: blocked, failed, stalled, or with an unreadable manifest;
  • a queued run refused at launch for a reason the coordinator must clear, with the remedy §74 composes;
  • a review finding that needs the coordinator: a blocking finding, or a dimension below the floor;
  • a limit reset reaching the session (the reset row of a-limit-reset-reaches-every-session §2);
  • a budget lift granted or ending that covers the session, drawn once per pane as today: the lead grants a lift in one session and relies on every session hearing it, so a coordinator can dispatch to the lane a lift opens and stop when it ends;
  • a declared wait a worker addresses to the coordinator;
  • the follower itself ending with no session host to take over.

Each such line is written once, when it becomes true, as that run's own row. The needs you: summary line is removed from the stream altogether. The lead does not want it: it addresses the orchestrator rather than the lead, it repeats rows the stream already carries, and the obligations hook restates the same list on every prompt. There is no reminder cadence either. Everything else leaves the stream and stays in crew view and the pane's history: launches, working/waiting transitions, landed and dropped rows the coordinator's own merge or discard caused, baseline inventory replayed after a re-arm or reload, reload notices, and the empty and drained summary lines §82 removes. The rule is stated once, in the module that decides what the stream carries, and both the row policy and the summary lines read it there.

Done when a test drives the follower over a stream carrying one event of every kind above, and finds a line for exactly the eight actionable kinds, each once. A delivered run prints its own row once and no needs you: line ever, and a twelve-item set of outstanding runs held unchanged for 35 minutes prints nothing more. A launch, a working/waiting change, the coordinator's own merge, and a re-arm baseline print nothing to the stream, and still appear in crew view. The negative control restores the needs-you line, and the delivered run then prints it as well as its row. The test files importing the changed modules add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. tests/test_every_module_fits_a_worker_window.py passes at head. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: reckon/crew/ticker.py, reckon/crew/needs_you.py, reckon/crew_follow_commands.py, and the tests that assert the rows this removes from the stream, named from the failure ids.

§84 — Stopping a run ends its whole process tree, so a discard succeeds

Why. Reported on 2026-10-22 by power_convertor session a2286ca9 (times UTC+2 unless marked Z). Twice the coordinator stopped an unwanted review with crew stop and then ran crew discard. Each time the discard was refused with recorded pid … is alive, because the run's srun wrapper had survived the SIGTERM (pids 1924345 and 2865968). A retry a minute later succeeded.

Deliverable. First reproduce a wrapper that survives the stop. Then crew stop resolves the run's whole process tree to pids and prints them, as the workstation's kill rule requires. It signals that list with SIGTERM, and after a bounded grace escalates to SIGKILL for whatever is still alive. It reports any survivor by pid. It never signals a pid outside the run's own tree, so a discard after a stop finds no live pid.

Done when a test with a fake run tree, a parent and a child wrapper that ignores SIGTERM, finds the stop ending both and printing their pids, a following discard succeeding, and an unrelated process left untouched. The negative control drops the escalation, and the discard is then refused. The test files importing the modules changed add no failure against a base arm. Both arms come from git checkouts under the run's own scratch directory, with the four follower variables unset, and run in place on the fleet node with pytest -n 8. Every log is under the run directory, starts with its revision and the literal command, and ends in EXIT=. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings. Write paths: the module that implements crew stop, named in the manifest, and the new test.

§85 — A discarded run is never listed as blocked

Why. Reported on 2026-10-22 by power_convertor session a2286ca9 (times UTC+2 unless marked Z). After the coordinator discarded an unwanted review, the pane went on naming it as needs you: 1 blocked review-of-… (04:10 and 06:16), and drew a halted → dropped row for a run already gone.

Deliverable. A run carrying a discard record classifies as dropped, never as blocked, and leaves the needs-you set and the obligations list the moment it is discarded. The stop that precedes a discard draws no halted row.

Done when a test stops and discards a run, and finds it absent from the needs-you set and the obligations list, with no halted row drawn. §86 — The obligations snapshot is fresh, or says why it is not

Deliverable. First find which process holds the producer seat when watch --ensure reports seat-held, and why that holder does not write the session's obligations snapshot while the pane still receives rows. Then repair it, so the seat holder writes the snapshot. Where a snapshot is stale, the hook names the reason: the seat holder's pid and host, and the age of its last write.

Done when the manifest names the cause, with the responsible line read from the committed blob and a reproduction. A test finds the snapshot refreshed within one producer interval by the seat holder, and the hook's stale notice naming the holder and its last write. §87 — Every unreconciled run is an obligation with a remedy

One of them, r-20261021T142127162278-plan-review-of-coil-supply-report-rebuild, had phase complete and a live pointer, was never listed as an obligation, and was offered no promote command. Deliverable. The unreconciled count and the obligations list come from one derivation, so every run counted carries an item with its remedy. A complete plan-review run with a live pointer yields its promote command.

§88 — A worker that ends its first turn without acting is named as such

The pane gave no hint that this was a premature end of turn rather than a failure, and the coordinator resumed it by hand. The workstation's guidance names this signature, one turn with no tool use and one text block, as distinct from a failure.

§89 — A resume that delivers at the same head is reviewed at that head

Why. §81 made the review reflex wait while a resumed run still carries the head its resume superseded. supersedes_head (reckon/crew/recovery_review_dispatch.py, near line 90) is true whenever the run's head equals the resume_superseded_head the pointer recorded. That is right while the resumed worker is still running. It is wrong when the resumed attempt finishes without a new commit. On 2026-10-23 additional-work-is-a-section §4, run r-20261023T012524464845-a-plan-write-refuses-the-bucket, was resumed at 03:31:24Z only to record the coordinator's population arms in its manifest. The worker rewrote the manifest to complete at 03:33:39Z at the same head, 6d14eefbc. The reflex then refused to compose its review ("was resumed and still carries 6d14eefbc…, the head its resume superseded"). The obligations derivation, which reads the same predicate, listed no review-missing duty for it either. So a finished run sat unreviewed and invisible for hours.

Deliverable. A resumed attempt that has delivered settles the superseded head, even at the same revision. A resume resets a delivered manifest's status to in-progress (resumption._reset_delivered_manifest_status). A manifest that reads a delivered status, complete or failed, and was written after the resume instant recorded in resume_superseded_head.at, is therefore the resumed attempt's own delivery. supersedes_head returns false for such a run. The review reflex then composes the review for the head the run carries, and the obligations derivation lists the duty again. A resumed run whose manifest has not been rewritten to a delivered status since the resume is withheld, as §81 requires.

Done when tests/test_a_resume_that_delivers_at_the_same_head_is_reviewed.py builds a temporary home, with every environment-resolved directory pointed at temporary paths, and holds all of these:

  • a run resumed at an instant, whose manifest is rewritten to complete after it with the head unchanged, gets a review composed for that head by dispatch_review_for_run in dry run, and the obligations derivation lists its review duty;
  • the same run with its manifest still in-progress after the resume is withheld, with the §81 reason;
  • a manifest whose complete was written before the resume instant is still withheld;
  • a run whose head moved after the resume is reviewed at its new head, as at base.

Negative control: drop the manifest check from supersedes_head, and the first case is withheld. The gate runs the new test with every test file that imports reckon.crew.recovery_review_dispatch or names resume_superseded_head, at base and at head, judged by delta. Each log names its revision and the literal command and ends in EXIT=, and baseline_suite and after_suite record failure_ids as pytest node ids. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the staged paths. Write paths: reckon/crew/recovery_review_dispatch.py, tests/test_a_resume_that_delivers_at_the_same_head_is_reviewed.py, docs/evidence/fragments/the-monitor-shows-the-work-it-watches/a-resume-that-delivers-at-the-same-head-is-reviewed.html.

§90 — A session id quoted in a refusal is not a refusal of the session

Why. On 2026-10-23 this session's queued run r-20261023T131811711592-work-outside-scope-returns-to-the-coordinator was refused at admission by an UnreconciledRuns refusal (reckon/crew/node.py, near line 455). The refusal named another run of the session whose review was in flight, and it quoted that review's dispatch command, which carries --session <id>. _queued_failure_remedy (reckon/crew/recovery_classification.py, near line 116) treats a refusal as a condition of the session whenever the session id appears anywhere in its text (near line 151). So the obligations view listed the run as queued-awaiting-session and published reckon crew follow as the remedy. The session's follower was alive throughout, and no follower clears an unreconciled-runs refusal. The refusal names its own two remedies: reconcile each named run, or pass the waiver. A queued run cannot take the waiver after it is queued, so the coordinator's only path was to discard the queued run and dispatch again with --allow-unreconciled-runs. The view named neither remedy.

Deliverable. A queued refusal is read as a condition of the session only when the session is the refusal's subject, not when a command quoted inside it carries the id. Tell the two apart by the exception class name the admission pass stamps at the head of the stored failure (<ClassName>: <text>), never by a substring of its whole text. That name is the only kind on record: format_refusal (reckon/crew/refusals.py, near line 98) appends the registered remedy but stores no refusal code. UnreconciledRuns takes the run-action remedy below. The follower refusal keeps the follower remedy. It is stamped as the private subclass _FollowerAdmissionUnmet (reckon/crew/dispatch_watch.py, near line 805), so the match names it beside its public base WatcherRequired. An unreconciled-runs refusal on a queued run publishes the first named run's own action, read from its - <run_id>: <action> line, the form UnreconciledRuns renders each run in (reckon/crew/node.py, near line 462). _remedy_command_stated_by (near line 99) reads only backticked spans, so on this refusal it returns the waiver reckon crew dispatch --allow-unreconciled-runs, the one remedy a queued run cannot take; it is not the reader for this kind. Its detail states that a queued run cannot take the waiver, so the alternative is to discard it and dispatch again with --allow-unreconciled-runs. A refusal whose subject is the session keeps the follower remedy it publishes today, for example a follower that is not registered.

Done when tests/test_a_quoted_session_id_is_not_a_session_refusal.py holds all of these:

  • a queued pointer whose failure is the text of an UnreconciledRuns the test raises itself, naming one run whose next action is a review dispatch command carrying the pointer's own session id, publishes that run's action as its next_command. It publishes neither the follower attach line nor the waiver. The run stays under queued-awaiting-session, because the session does owe the fix: _queued_failure_remedy's result reaches the obligations view through session_refusal (recovery_classification.py, near line 738), and both producers list any refusal carried there under that kind (obligations.py, near line 1342; obligation_snapshot.py, near line 1042). The case is asserted on the classified row's session_refusal and on the duty the obligations view builds from it. The fixture builds the failure only through the class, never as hand-written text;
  • the classified row's session_refusal detail names discarding the queued run and dispatching again with --allow-unreconciled-runs. The assertion is on the classified row, because the obligations duty row carries no detail field (DUTY_ROW_FIELDS, reckon/crew/obligations.py, near line 78);
  • a queued pointer refused for a condition whose subject is the session still publishes the follower attach line under queued-awaiting-session.

Negative control: restore the substring test at line 151, and the first case fails. The gate runs the new test with tests/test_a_queued_refusal_names_its_remedy.py, tests/test_a_session_side_refusal_reaches_the_session.py, tests/test_crew_promotion.py and every test file that imports reckon.crew.recovery_classification or reckon.crew.obligations, at base and at head, judged by delta. Each log names its revision and the literal command and ends in EXIT=, and baseline_suite and after_suite record failure_ids as pytest node ids. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the staged paths. Write paths: reckon/crew/recovery_classification.py, tests/test_a_quoted_session_id_is_not_a_session_refusal.py, docs/evidence/fragments/the-monitor-shows-the-work-it-watches/a-quoted-session-id-is-not-a-session-refusal.html.

§91 — A disposition recorded after promotion reaches the reflex that reads it

Why. On 2026-10-23, run r-20261023T120032911615-a-dispatch-asks-git-only-about-claims-that-could-meet-its-own was repaired after a review raised a blocking finding. Its re-review then could not be composed by the reflex or by hand, because two readers disagreed about the same review:

  • The review run was promoted, and the coordinator then recorded all four findings as folded. The dispositions landed on the committed record under docs/state/reckon/reviews/run/<run>/.
  • dispatch_review_for_run (reckon/crew/recovery_review_dispatch.py, near line 2395) first asks _open_review_refusal (near line 2276). That reads stored_records_for_run (reckon/crew/review_records.py, near line 664), which returns only the staging copy under ~/.config/reckon/crew/reviews/reckon/, unmerged. The staging copy was last written before the dispositions, so the refusal reported finding 0 as blocking with no disposition, and refused the round.
  • The dispose command it printed then refused too: the run's head had moved past the reviewed head with the repair.

Neither path could clear it; the round was composed only by calling the text-review dispatch below the refusal. The coordinator ran the same reader at the revision before the review store's incremental index landed and got the same answer, so the defect predates that change. The merge that would have prevented it already exists: _record_with_merged_answers (near line 459) unions every copy's answers, staging and committed, through _merge_record_answers, which carries finding_dispositions (near line 128). stored_records_for_run does not apply it.

Deliverable. stored_records_for_run returns each record carrying the answers held by every copy of the same review run, staging and committed, merged by _record_with_merged_answers, so finding_dispositions and dimension_dispositions are included. Its other readers then see the same answers the refusal does: obligations.py, needs_you.py, promotion_checks.py, review.py and review_lifecycle.py. Which records are returned, and their order, are unchanged.

Done when tests/test_a_disposition_after_promotion_reaches_the_reflex.py builds a temporary review store, with every environment-resolved directory pointed at temporary paths, and holds all of these:

  • a staging record for a run at one head carries a blocking finding, and a committed copy of the same review run carries that finding's disposition. stored_records_for_run returns the record with the disposition.
  • with the run's live pointer at a later head, dispatch_review_for_run(..., dry_run=True) is not refused by _open_review_refusal.
  • a disposition recorded only on the staging copy is still returned. A record with no answers on any copy is returned unchanged, and the records and their order match the unmerged reader's.

Negative control: return the staging record unmerged, and the first two cases fail. The gate runs the new test with every test file that imports reckon.crew.review_records, reckon.crew.recovery_review_dispatch or reckon.crew.review_lifecycle, at base and at head, judged by delta. Each log names its revision and the literal command and ends in EXIT=, and baseline_suite and after_suite record failure_ids as pytest node ids. ruff check and ruff format --check add no findings on the staged paths. Write paths: reckon/crew/review_records.py, tests/test_a_disposition_after_promotion_reaches_the_reflex.py, docs/evidence/fragments/the-monitor-shows-the-work-it-watches/a-disposition-after-promotion-reaches-the-reflex.html.

§ Decisions

When does the plugin monitor start?

A plugin monitor can start at session start ( always ) or the first time a named skill runs. Plan reviews dispatched from /reckon-create and /reckon-edit need a follower as much as /reckon-build runs do. (Locked on a-follower-lives-as-long-as-its-session; carried here when that plan was folded in on 2026-10-13.)

The first dispatch is what needs a follower, whichever skill or tool makes it. A skill list would miss review workers and any dispatch made through the CLI or MCP. The cost is one idle shell per interactive session, waiting on a node-local FIFO.

What happens where no host is running?

(Locked on a-follower-lives-as-long-as-its-session; carried here when that plan was folded in on 2026-10-13.)

Codex sessions, -p sessions and sessions without the plugin have no plugin monitor, so the current mechanism stays for them unchanged.

monitor-stream-is-actionable-only

Under the session host every printed line starts a coordinator turn; a line that names nothing to do costs a turn and teaches the coordinator to ignore the stream.

What does the host print to the pane?

(Locked on a-follower-lives-as-long-as-its-session; carried here when that plan was folded in on 2026-10-13.)

The pane stays the ticker it is today. Host status goes to its log and record, never to stdout, consistent with the rule that the stream carries worker transitions and fleet posture and nothing else.

pane-end

A plugin monitor that exits is never restarted in the session. Claude Code starts one only at session start or plugin reload ('always'), or the first time a named skill runs ('on-skill-invoke'), and no hook, skill or CLI command can restart it. So 'host exits' would leave a resumed session with no pane. The host stays alive and the follower child settles on an idle line, and the next dispatch restarts the child through the host's request pipe at no turn cost. The cost is that Claude Code still shows the monitor as running.

What replaces q for the waiting counter, given that the compact letters are a display contract readers have memorised?

(Locked on monitor-renders-what-it-is-given; carried here when that plan was folded in on 2026-10-13.)

None of the three offered options was taken. The counter spells the word queued rather than carrying any single letter, and the ambiguity disappears because a spelled word needs no mapping to learn. The decision also explains the letter it replaces: q was never arbitrary, it is the first letter of queued — a word the surface never showed anywhere. So the compact rule was intact all along and the display was withholding the term the rule was derived from, which is this sprint's subject rather than an exception to it. Spelling it out costs width in the one cell where the reader most needs certainty, and removes the collision with working without re-lettering a counter readers have used far longer.

Once a follower sweeps only its own session, who sweeps the runs of a session that has no follower?

(Locked on a-follower-keeps-your-work-in-view; carried here when that plan was folded in on 2026-10-13.)

Measured 2026-09-22 over seven days and four projects. A coordinator that is present re-arms fast: median 12 seconds, 471 of 587 gaps under a minute, 90th percentile 9.7 minutes. The tail, 99th percentile 5.2 hours and maximum 12.9 hours, is coordinators who were absent, and 13 expiries were never re-armed; three sessions were unattended at the census with pointers 24 to 26 hours old. A grace period of ten minutes, just above the 90th percentile, leaves a present coordinator's re-arm alone and sweeps the sessions nobody is watching, while the 99th percentile would have disabled the sweep exactly when it is needed. The coordinator's own session showed this the same night: its follower was down about two and a half hours during a usage-limit pause, and two finished runs received no review dispatch. No live pointer or registration named another host, so treating a foreign-host pointer as unknown costs nothing today. The watcher records itself as the writer of each sweep action, because no record today carries the sweeping session.

One monitor plan. At the lead's request this plan now holds every open monitor section. §7–§11 were §2–§6 of a-follower-keeps-your-work-in-view (S22), §12 was §10 of monitor-renders-what-it-is-given (S22), and §13–§14 were §9 and §16 of a-follower-lives-as-long-as-its-session (S25). Their locked decisions are carried in the decisions block, and a reference inside a folded section to its old plan's numbering means the section it became here. That plan's §14 is landed though declared deferred, so it was not carried. §15–§16 are new: the pane's flicker, measured on 2026-10-13 as 61% of all transitions. crew-observability-truth stays separate, because its open sections are about fleet truth (gates, lanes, gc), not the pane.

admit_once in reckon/crew/queue_admission.py asks the lane's gate document (_dispatch_lane_gate) and a dependency-and-back-off guard before releasing a queued pointer, so a request kept by the lane or by a not-yet-aged non-lane-hold failure keeps its run id and queued phase and appends no transition; the guard fingerprints the request's brief, its plan and the flight configuration layers (reckon.flight host and project layers), never the router's republished lane document; the first failure records failure_reported_at and appends one row to the project's watch stream, once; tests/test_a_held_request_stays_queued.py 6 passed, negative control 2 failed (EXIT=1).

§15 landed. The queue runner no longer withdraws and re-queues a request that the lane holds. It backs off a request that cannot launch until that request's brief, plan or flight configuration changes, and reports the failure once. Merged b000cdc72 as 02f1c6274. The gate passed with 6 tests, 18 in the importing selection, and was replayed green at the integrated head. Review: 94, with 0 added failures. Follow-on: _file_stamp duplicates capabilities.file_stamp. evidence

Noted for §16's node. A budget lift scoped to one session (on 2026-10-13, codex-sub scoped to session:b8e6ba21…) emits its lift-granted, lift-in-force and lift-ended rows with no session, so every pane draws them. They are rare, 4 of 645 events in an hour, and not part of the flicker. A lift scoped to a session should carry that session on its rows, so the row policy can hold it on other sessions' panes as observer context.

A further round trip measured on 2026-10-14, beyond the requeue kinds this section names. Run r-20261014T090025554907 was launched on clu-2002 and was writing its stream throughout. The pane drew it working → halted at 09:04:30Z and halted → working at 09:04:55Z. The producer's row carried to_state: running and classification: running, but also recovery_classification: unwritten, liveness_proven: false and recovery: resume. The producer runs on clu-2018, so local_liveness correctly leaves a clu-2002 pid unproven. With no manifest yet, the recovery word becomes unwritten, which PANE_STATES files under halted. The worker wrote its first manifest at 09:04:54Z, and the row returned. So every run launched on a host other than the producer's draws this round trip before its first manifest write. This section's settle window would hold it, since the return came 25 s later. Its stated order is met: §6 is done, and the-suite-is-green-at-head §15 merged at ce8f25bc6. Separately, offering recovery: resume for an unproven run whose stream is fresh invites a second writer from any coordinator on a host that cannot see the pid. §82 landed at 476acc24d: the empty needs-you line and the drained settle line are gone. Reviewed 100/120. The non-empty needs-you reminder stays until §83. evidence

Coordinator ruling during the build, 2026-10-22. The worker found that two coordinator kinds render with the waiting word, and that the halt tests' fixtures include launch and working-to-waiting rows. A row is kept or dropped by what it asks of the coordinator, not by its state word. A row that moves a run to waiting because it needs the coordinator, whether an audited coordinator kind or a declared wait addressed to the coordinator, stays in the stream. A launch, or a working/waiting change that asks nothing, leaves it. In the halt tests only the fixture expectations for launch and plain working/waiting rows are re-pointed, and every halt assertion stays as it is.

§83's rule for launch, resume and working/waiting rows is superseded by a-live-worker-is-never-drawn-halted §14 (commit d58ae6a22). That follows the lead's 2026-10-23 direction: “we want oversight on state changes. A review that is launched is a state change, a job that switches from implemented to review is a state change. Yes decrease noise, but keep signal.” The stream will carry each run's launch, a resume, and “delivered → in review” on the reviewed run. The flicker, baseline and same-word holds that §83 introduced stay. The withhold already on main (ticker.stream_carries, reckon/crew/ticker.py near line 1195, from 06467f9da) is §14's to change.

The section is declared deferred, so nothing further is built under its old rule. Its blocked run, r-20261022T125645619174-the-stream-carries-only-actionable-lines, belongs to session f653840f, which could not be reached. crew view directory lists that session as all-terminal with no live node. The only transport it records is a fenced worker's socket, not a coordinator's address. The run is left for the lead to dispose.
